114	1. **vc-x1 push: support new cycle protocol shape (N:1 code↔bot).**
115	   Today push assumes 1:1 symmetric WC commits with shared
116	   title/body. The new cycle protocol has a different shape on
117	   each side:
118	   - code side: fully committed before push (N commits via
119	     merge), nothing to commit at push time
120	   - `.claude` side: one commit with its own message (distinct
121	     from any code commit) and a multi-line `ochid:` listing
122	     all N code commits
123	
124	   Teach push to:
125	   - detect this shape
126	   - skip `commit-app` when code WC is empty
127	   - compose a `.claude`-specific message
128	   - emit multi-line `ochid:` per the design in [[10]]
129	
130	   Today's workaround: pre-commit `.claude` manually, then
131	   `vc-x1 push <bm> --from bookmark-both --yes`.
132	2. **vc-x1 push --squash: symmetric squash on both repos.**
133	   Automate Option F (manually exercised in 0.59.0
134	   close-out): app-side squash + bot-side description
135	   rewrite + force-push, atomically. Without this,
136	   squash is a manual recipe that future cycles must
137	   follow each time.
138	   - App side: squash cycle commits into one new commit;
139	     capture the squashed chid.

1	# Max review #1 — 0.61.0 por/dual parity design
2	
3	Captured 2026-05-26 (max effort) after the close-out commit
4	of 0.61.0 (`c0f3376f`, pre-push to `main`). Independent
5	review of the design output (audit doc, copying stub,
6	chores narrative). Each item below carries a **Status** —
7	open (needs decision), resolved (with the agreed
8	disposition), or applied (already changed).
9	
10	## Scope reviewed
11	
12	- [`por-dual-parity-audit.md`](por-dual-parity-audit.md) —
13	  canonical design doc post-0.61.0 (≈1090 lines).
14	- [`copying.md`](copying.md) — copying mechanism design
15	  stub from `0.61.0-4`.
16	- [`por-dual-parity.md`](por-dual-parity.md) — older
17	  forward-looking doc (read for context only; not
18	  critiqued).
19	- [`/notes/chores/chores-12.md > ## docs: por/dual parity
20	  design (0.61.0)`](../chores/chores-12.md#docs-pordual-parity-design-0610) —
21	  cycle narrative.
22	
23	Line numbers in this review are **as of the 0.61.0
24	close-out snapshot**; section / heading names are the
25	durable references.
26	
27	## Strengths
28	
29	For balance — what the bot thinks works well:
30	
31	- **Three-pass methodology** (audit → commonality
32	  inversion → axis decomposition). The `-2` inversion is
33	  the cycle's pivotal move: reframed "we have a problem
34	  everywhere" to "the right pattern already exists, extend
35	  it." That mental flip made the rest tractable.
36	- **Axis decomposition is genuine decomposition.** Calling
37	  out `--por` as a bundle of six axes opens design space
38	  the bundle hid. The litmus is `audit.md:752` — "A user
39	  wanting a fully-plain single repo today can't spell it" —
40	  a real defect of the bundle framing.
41	- **A2 collapse was earned.** `.vc-config.toml` write was
42	  initially listed as independent; the design pass
43	  noticed presence-of-`.vc-config.toml` ≡ Topology and
44	  retired the axis. Honest housekeeping.
45	- **Two-class principle ("defaults where natural; errors
46	  only for user-specific keys") is sound.** Correct
47	  generalization of what `resolve_repo` already does.
48	  Baked-in default config + `vc-x1 config dump` closes
49	  the "what does layer-4-empty mean?" question
50	  elegantly.
51	- **T7-only promotion.** Resisting the urge to seed all
52	  14 Gaps into Todo is the right restraint — cheapest
53	  concrete equalization that validates the
54	  topology-from-config rule as the prototype.
55	- **Status note + "implementation will diverge" framing**
56	  (`audit.md:19-37`) is humility the bot will thank
57	  itself for in 0.65.0.
58	
59	## Concerns
60	
61	### 1. Runtime `--por` semantics — doc text vs. intent
62	
63	**Original critique** (max, 2026-05-26): The Topology
64	Decisions block at `audit.md:530-534` reads:
65	
66	> `--por` at runtime short-circuits `default_scope` →
67	> **`Scope([Code])`** regardless of what the workspace
68	> says. Escape hatch for "this command, just my code,
69	> leave `.claude/` alone."
70	
71	The bot read that as code-privileged: creation-time
72	`--por` means "this workspace is single," runtime `--por`
73	means "ignore the bot side this invocation." Two
74	different operations mounted on one spelling — felt
75	overloaded.
76	
77	**User clarification** (wink, 2026-05-26): runtime `--por`
78	isn't side-specific. It means **"treat the repo I'm
79	pointed at (`.` by default, or `-R/--repo <path>`) as a
80	single repo — no sibling lookup."** Symmetric across
81	code and bot:
82	
83	- `--por -R .` (in a dual workspace) → operate on code,
84	  no sibling.
85	- `--por -R .claude` → operate on `.claude`, no sibling.
86	- `--por -R /vendored/single-repo` → operate on that
87	  repo as-is.
88	
89	Creation-time `--por` is a special case of the same rule
90	(the target doesn't exist yet, so "treat as standalone"
91	collapses to "create as standalone"). One rule, two life
92	stages.
93	
94	**Bot retraction:** the "two meanings on one spelling"
95	critique evaporates under this framing. Also: the bot's
96	proposed alternative ("use `--scope code` instead") was
97	wrong — it conflates *selecting one side of a known dual
98	workspace* (`--scope`) with *declaring the target isn't
99	dual* (`--por`). Different mental models, not
100	interchangeable.
101	
102	**Status:** **resolved on framing; doc-text update
103	pending.** The audit doc currently codifies the narrower
104	code-asymmetric reading at two spots:
105	
106	- `audit.md:530-534` (Topology Decisions, "runtime
107	  override" bullet) — says `→ Scope([Code])`, "just my
108	  code."
109	- `audit.md:897-913` (Por's view of the chain, "Runtime
110	  `--por`" bullet) — says "short-circuits
111	  `default_scope` → `Scope([Code])` and ignores
112	  `.claude/`."
113	
114	**Action:** at the next pickup of this design (likely
115	0.62.0 Preparation), rewrite both spots to spell out the
116	target-relative semantics. Suggested text for the
117	Topology bullet:
118	
119	> Runtime override on every subcommand: `--por` declares
120	> the target repo (default `.`, or `-R/--repo <path>`)
121	> as single — no sibling traversal, no `.vc-config.toml
122	> > other-repo` lookup, no cross-repo ochid validation.
123	> Symmetric across code and bot — `--por` doesn't
124	> privilege a side, it suppresses the sibling-discovery
125	> step.
126	
127	### 2. "Exactly-one-of `--por` / `--dual` with default-dual" is contradictory phrasing
128	
129	**Critique:** `audit.md:521-522` says clap-enforced
130	exactly-one-of `--por` / `--dual`; `:525` says
131	default-dual. If neither flag is passed, either clap
132	rejects (true exactly-one-of, friction for the common
133	case) or it accepts (default kicks in, "exactly-one-of"
134	is misleading). Same shape on `--private` / `--public`
135	at `:643-647`.
136	
137	**Status:** open.
138	
139	**Proposed action:** spell it "**at most one of**;
140	absence → workspace-config or baked-in default." Honest
141	about what the default does. Same fix for Privacy. Docs-
142	only edit, no code implications.
143	
144	### 3. `--init-from-recursive` doubles the surface for the same capability
145	
146	**Critique:** `copying.md:29-38` defines six flags:
147	`--init-from`, `--init-from-code`, `--init-from-bot`,
148	and `-recursive` variants of each. But recursion is a
149	property of the *operand*, not the flag: `cp src/* dst/`
150	is recursive whether you said `-r` or not; `cp
151	src/foo.txt dst/` isn't. The shell expands the glob; the
152	bot thinks vc-x1 should walk each resolved path and do
153	the right thing.
154	
155	**Proposed action:** one flag per side.
156	`--init-from-code=<glob>` does the right thing per
157	resolved path (file → file copy; dir → recursive copy).
158	Cuts the surface in half, matches `cp` semantics users
159	already have in their head. The `-recursive` naming
160	muddies what the flag controls vs. what the operand
161	controls.
162	
163	**Status:** open. Material to copying.md design before
164	that cycle starts.
165	
166	### 4. `--repo none` × Topology interaction isn't spelled out
167	
168	**Critique:** the Remote axis Decisions
169	(`audit.md:596-624`) introduces `--repo none` as a peer
170	category, with `--account` skipped under it. Fine in
171	isolation. But `--repo none` × `--dual` means "dual
172	workspace with no remote for either side." The
173	bot/sync/push workflow assumes remote-backed `.claude/`.
174	The doc doesn't say whether dual+no-remote is
175	first-class or rejected at parse time.
176	
177	**Proposed action:** add one paragraph to the Remote
178	axis (or matrix footnotes) on the Topology × Remote=none
179	interaction. Don't leave it implicit. The bot thinks a
180	future user trying `init --dual --repo none` will hit a
181	confused error later in the workflow.
182	
183	**Status:** open. Affects implementation when `--repo
184	none` lands (Gap #5).
185	
186	### 5. List-valued CLI-vs-config "wins" semantics deserves an explicit callout
187	
188	**Critique:** `audit.md:697-699` says: "If user passes
189	any `--init-from*` on CLI, the config-pinned list is
190	ignored — match the broader chain rule."
191	
192	The "broader chain rule" is about *scalar* values where
193	"wins" unambiguously means "replaces." For list-valued
194	axes, the bot thinks users coming from `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`
195	/ `PATH` will expect merge, not replace. The replace
196	choice is defensible (predictability beats expressivity)
197	but it's not the obvious choice.
198	
199	**Proposed action:** keep the replace semantics, but add
200	an explicit "list-typed-axis rule" sentence — "for
201	list-valued axes like Copying, CLI replaces config; to
202	merge, pass the config globs on CLI too." Prevents the
203	same conversation in 0.62.0.
204	
205	**Status:** open. Docs-only edit.
206	
207	### 6. Gap-list ordering hides one structural dependency
208	
209	**Critique:** `audit.md:1010-1080` ranks gaps by "blast
210	radius." But Gap #7 (`default_scope` broken-dual
211	detection) is a *prerequisite* for Gap #9 (copying
212	mechanism), per `copying.md:81-92` — copying defers
213	validation to the first downstream subcommand, which
214	means broken-dual detection has to exist first.
215	
216	**Proposed action:** one-line note above the list:
217	"ranking is rough; some structural prerequisites (e.g.
218	#7 before #9) override size ordering." Doesn't require
219	renumbering.
220	
221	**Status:** open. Docs-only edit.
222	
223	## Nits
224	
225	Lower-priority items; do as time allows.
226	
227	- **`finalize` matrix row** marks T+SC support based on
228	  the body being topology-neutral. True, but no command
229	  path exercises it today. The bot thinks adding a
230	  footnote — "currently latent — body supports it, no
231	  caller surfaces it" — would prevent a future cycle
232	  hardening a use case nobody asked for.
233	
234	- **Reading guide at top of audit doc.** The doc is ≈1090
235	  lines and will be cited from many cycles. A `## Reading
236	  guide` (matrix → §X; one axis → §Y; new axis → §Z)
237	  costs 10 lines and pays back across many readings. The
238	  bot thinks this is worth doing before 0.62.0 starts.
239	
240	- **"Por's view of the chain"** (`audit.md:897-913`)
241	  duplicates the per-axis Decisions blocks. The bot
242	  thinks it could be dropped or reframed as a
243	  debugging cheat-sheet ("when does topology actually
244	  get consulted?") — a different angle.
245	
246	- **`validate-todo` / `fix-todo`** are topology-blind by
247	  design. Worth one sentence somewhere ("notes/-family
248	  commands operate outside the workspace shape") so a
249	  future reader doesn't suspect they were missed.
250	
251	## Process observation
252	
253	The user-proposed-then-deferred `~/.config/vc-x1/config.
254	toml` topology default that **opened** the cycle is, by
255	close-out, **in** the design (`audit.md:763`:
256	`[default].topology = "single" | "dual"`). The bot's
257	initial "audit first, expose defaults later" pushback
258	was about *ordering*, not the idea itself. Net: the cycle
259	delivered what the user originally proposed, just through
260	a more thorough process.
261	
262	The bot thinks this is worth noting because it shows the
263	pushback shape ("don't ship that *yet*") working
264	correctly — but also that the original ask was the right
265	ask. The Decisions blocks currently read as if the design
266	emerged from the bot's analysis alone; an acknowledgment
267	of the original user framing would balance the record.
268	
269	**Status:** open. Optional chores narrative tweak (no
270	action implied for the design itself).
271

506	#### Topology
507	
508	- **States** — `single` (one code repo) | `dual` (code repo

511	  is the boolean opt-out. No `--dual` peer.
512	- **Surface** — `--por` (boolean, `init` and `clone` only).
513	  Downstream commands infer from `.vc-config.toml >
514	  [workspace] other-repo` via `default_scope`.
515	- **Gap** — `--dual` doesn't exist as a peer flag, so the
516	  defaulting is asymmetric: you can be explicit about por
517	  but not about dual.

519	**Decisions (0.61.0):**
520	
521	- **Add `--dual` peer flag**. `--por` / `--dual`
522	  There are two possible values --por or --dual
523	  --por is Plan Old Repo and when used the target
524	  is treated as a regular repo.

535	  dropped.
536	- **Resolution chain**: CLI > ENV > Local > Global >
537	  baked-in default (`dual`). No "Error" floor for
538	  Topology — baked-in always supplies.

> AGENT

Two spots are flagged; here's what I suggest for each.

**Spot 1** — `audit.md:529-532` (Topology Decisions, "Runtime override" bullet). Keep it tight, matches the surrounding bullets:

```
- **Runtime override on every subcommand** (not just
  `init` / `clone`). `--por` declares the target repo
  (`.` by default, or `-R/--repo <path>`) as single —
  no sibling traversal, no `[workspace] other-repo`
  lookup, no cross-repo ochid validation. Symmetric
  across code and bot — `--por` suppresses sibling
  discovery for whichever repo is the target, it
  doesn't privilege a side.
```

**Spot 2** — `audit.md:907-911` ("Por's view of the chain"). This subsection is explanatory rather than decisive, so examples earn their space here:

```
- **Runtime `--por`** — overrides workspace topology
  detection across every subcommand (not just
  `init`/`clone`). Treats the target repo (`.` by
  default, or `-R/--repo <path>`) as single: no sibling
  traversal, no `[workspace] other-repo` lookup, no
  cross-repo ochid validation. Symmetric across code
  and bot:
  - `--por -R .` (in a dual workspace) → operate on
    code, no sibling.
  - `--por -R .claude` → operate on `.claude`, no
    sibling.
  - `--por -R /vendored/single-repo` → operate on that
    repo as-is.
```

**Why this split**: Decisions blocks elsewhere in this section are 3-5 line bullets without nested examples — keeping Spot 1 in that shape preserves scannability. The "Por's view" subsection is the natural home for the concrete examples that make the symmetry click.

Found a **third spot** the review missed. Lines 985–995 — the matrix's "Topology column quick reference":

```
- *Runtime override* (other subcommands): `--por` at
  invocation short-circuits `default_scope` →
  `Scope([Code])` regardless of workspace contents.
  Default-dual still applies at the workspace level
  (the `.vc-config.toml` says dual, but the user is
  asking for "this command only, just my code").
```

Same misread — explicitly says "just my code." Proposed rewrite:

```
- *Runtime override* (other subcommands): `--por`
  treats the target repo (`.` by default, or
  `-R/--repo <path>`) as single — no sibling
  traversal, no `[workspace] other-repo` lookup.
  Symmetric across code and bot. Default-dual at the
  workspace level still applies (`.vc-config.toml`
  still says dual); `--por` is a per-invocation
  override asking "treat the target as single for
  this command."
```

**False positives I checked and skipped** (all describe correct behavior, not the misread):
- `:168`, `:272`, `:318` — `default_scope` returning `Scope([Code])` when there's no workspace root or no `other-repo` key. That's correct in a por workspace (no `.claude/` exists, so scope IS just `Code`). Describes mechanism, not `--por` semantics.
- `:173` — "a por run still operates on cwd" — describes today's sync behavior; cwd IS the target in a por workspace. Consistent with target-relative framing.
- `:1045` — broken-dual fallback to `Scope([Code])` is described as a footgun and is the subject of Gap #7's fix. Already flagged for change.

**Copying.md is clean** — no asymmetric phrasing.

Not gibberish — that's a clean snapshot pattern. Let me sketch what I think you mean and confirm before doing it.

Current `@` (uncommitted) holds three things:
1. `max-review-1.md` — with my Status/Disposition edits I'd revert.
2. `por-dual-parity-audit.md` — the 4 applied rewrites (Concern #1 ×3 spots, Concern #2 Topology).
3. `todo.md` — the Ideas #2 entry.

Two ways to read "commit that file":

**A. Single mixed commit (simpler).** Revert (1) to pristine, then `jj commit` capturing all three together (pristine critique + applied changes + ideas), then `jj new`. One snapshot. The critique-as-written is preserved at that commit; future deletions in `max-review-1.md` diff against it.

**B. Split: max-review-1.md alone (cleaner separation).** Revert (1), then `jj split` to put pristine `max-review-1.md` in its own commit and the audit/todo changes in another, then `jj new`. The captured critique sits alone in a commit titled "snapshot: max review #1 as captured" or similar; the applications are a separate sibling commit.

A few notes either way:

- **Reverting (1) — no git history to restore from.** `max-review-1.md` is `A` in git status (never committed), so I'd reverse my 4 edits manually rather than `jj restore`. I have the diffs in this session's record.
- **This sits outside cycle protocol.** You said "no formal set of git changes" — so neither A nor B follows the cycle ladder (`X.Y.Z-N` titles, per-commit `cargo fmt`/`clippy`/`test`/`install`). For docs-only edits the install step is skip-able per protocol, so the cost is mainly the unconventional title. We'd plan to fold this into a proper cycle later (likely 0.62.0).
- **`jj new` after either** creates an empty `@` on top; future per-item resolutions (delete applied sections from `max-review-1.md`, plus audit-doc edits, plus todo.md updates) accumulate there.

I lean **A** — simpler, fewer steps, and a single snapshot commit is the lowest overhead given this is informal. **B** earns its complexity only if we want the pristine capture isolated from any applied changes, which we don't really need (git can show `max-review-1.md` at any commit).

Which one? Or am I still reading it wrong?

```
277	### Shape at close-out push
278	
279	Decide the shape and get user approval before pushing —
280	the choice is hard to undo once on the target. Surface
281	the options, wait for the user.
282	
283	- **Squash to one commit** — single entry on the target.
284	  Right for straightforward changes where the Work-N is
285	  focused on one or two files.
286	- **Merge non-ff** — `main` gains a merge commit
287	  (`X.Y.Z`); cycle commits stay reachable via two
288	  parents. `jj log -r ..@ -n <N>` shows the trapezoidal
289	  shape. Set up: `jj rebase -r <tip> --onto <prev>
290	  --onto <sub-tip>` where `<prev>` is the previous
291	  cycle's closed-out commit (current main tip). Two
292	  `--onto` → `<tip>` becomes a merge of `<prev>` +
293	  `<sub-tip>`.
294	- **Keep separate** — one commit per cycle entry on
295	  `main`. Use when the decomposition itself is
296	  informative. Each chores section keeps its own header /
297	  `Commits:` ref; no consolidation churn.
298	
299	Set up squash/merge before invoking `vc-x1 push`; use
300	`jj git push` directly for non-standard shapes.
301	
302	### vc-x1 push wrapper
303	
304	`vc-x1 push <bookmark>` wraps per-push mechanics. See
305	`vc-x1 push --help` for current flags.
306	
307	**Current limitation**: only fully supports the
308	[Keep separate](#shape-at-close-out-push) shape; other
309	shapes need manual jj steps. Improvements tracked /
310	planned:
311	
312	- N:1 code↔bot for Merge non-ff (`## Todo` entry P1).
313	- Symmetric squash (planned, to be captured in `-2`).
314	- Per-repo bookmark names (planned).
315	
316	### .claude cadence
317	
318	**Cadence**: one push = one `.claude` commit, paired
319	with every code commit in that push.
320	
321	The `.claude` working copy accumulates session data
322	across the cycle; its change ID stays stable across
323	snapshots, `jj describe`, and the finalize commit, so
324	code-side `ochid:` trailers resolve.
325	
326	`.claude` is a linear journal — all session work lives
327	on `main`, regardless of the app-side bookmark. **Do not
328	create or maintain `.claude` bookmarks that mirror
329	app-side branches** — risks the bot steering session
330	pushes to the wrong remote ref.
331	
332	### Bot communication at the reviews
333	
334	Use plain prose — no insider jargon ("Gate N signal",
335	"Checkpoint N", etc.):
336	
337	- **At Work review** — summarize what changed and stop.
338	  "Work complete. Please review."
339	- **At Commit Description review** — present `$TITLE`
340	  and `$BODY` explicitly; ask permission to commit/push.
341	  Don't spell out the full `vc-x1 push ... --title ...
342	  --body ...` invocation by default.
343	- **At Post close-out review** — surface the shape
344	  options (squash / merge / keep) and the push target;
345	  wait for the user's choice before any `jj squash` /
346	  `jj rebase` / `jj git push` invocation.
347	
348	### After finalize: stop and wait
349	
350	After `vc-x1 finalize` launches (mid-session per-push or
351	at session-end), you **MUST NEVER** proceed (next step,
352	edit, tool call, text output) until the user explicitly
353	directs you to continue. **Even when the next step seems
354	obvious — wait.** Treat finalize as a hard stop for the
355	whole turn; any final words go in the approval prompt
356	*before* executing finalize. Auto-proceeding bypasses
357	the push+finalize checkpoint the user controls.
358	
359	### Recovery
360	
361	- **If push exits before `finalize-claude`** (e.g.
362	  failure between `push-app` and `finalize-claude`), run
363	  finalize by hand:
364	
365	  ```
366	  vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
```

> AGENT

Honest assessment: **doable, but not "1-command easy"** given our current state.

**State**:
- 10 unpushed code commits (0.61.0-0..-7 + close-out `c0f3376f` + snapshot `604fb9e8`).
- All 0.61.0 cycle commits share one ochid → `.claude` change `yvqzvvvuyvqv` (one session for the whole cycle).
- `.claude` `yvqzvvvuyvqv` has no description set — it's been a mutable `@` the whole cycle.
- Snapshot commit `604fb9e8` has **no ochid trailer** (informally committed via raw `jj` rather than `vc-x1 push`).

**Gotchas for merge non-ff**:
1. **`vc-x1 push` wrapper doesn't fully support merge non-ff** per `cycle-protocol.md > vc-x1 push wrapper` — manual `jj git push` needed for the close-out push.
2. **`.claude` side needs the `yvqzvvvuyvqv` change described** before push (it's been an unnamed running session).
3. **Snapshot `604fb9e8` lives above c0f3376f** — jj should auto-rebase it on top of the new merge (it stays local since `main` will only point at the merge). Good.
4. **Bookmark `main`**: at `41ef884` (0.60.0); needs to move to the new merge commit.

**Recipe sketch** (5 steps, all reversible until the `git push`):

```
# 1. Rewrite c0f3376f as a merge of 41ef884 + 587749d
jj rebase -r worlryqm --onto 41ef884 --onto 587749d -R .

# 2. Point app main at the new merge
jj bookmark set main -r worlryqm -R .

# 3. Describe the .claude session for 0.61.0
jj describe -r yvqzvvvuyvqv -m "..." -m "..." -R .claude

# 4. Point .claude main at the finalized session
jj bookmark set main -r yvqzvvvuyvqv -R .claude

# 5. Push both sides
jj git push --bookmark main -R .
jj git push --bookmark main -R .claude
```

The .claude description (step 3) is the trickiest part — it should be one message describing the whole cycle session (the bot has been driving 0.61.0 across many code commits but `.claude` is just one change).

**Easier alternative**: keep-separate push via the wrapper (`vc-x1 push`). Gets the same recovery point, locks in linear public history instead of trapezoidal. Mechanically much simpler since the wrapper handles bookmark/finalize/pairing.

Want to proceed with merge non-ff, or switch to keep-separate for the recovery push?

60	## Preparation
61	
62	The cycle's first commit (`X.Y.Z-0`):
63	
64	- **Backfill the previous cycle's chores section
65	  `Commits:` ref** — see
66	  [Chores commit references](../CLAUDE.md#chores-commit-references).
67	- **Bump the version** in `Cargo.toml` to `X.Y.Z-0`.
68	- **Pick up a `## Todo` item** (if the cycle has one).
69	  Move into `## In Progress` as:
70	  - A **bold title line** — exact match of the chores
71	    section header, minus the `## ` prefix.
72	  - A **succinct problem statement**.
73	  - A **plan ladder**.
74	
75	  See [Prose form](../CLAUDE.md#prose-form). A Todo that
76	  duplicates current `## In Progress` work is a process
77	  bug.
78	- **Open the [chores section](#chores-sections)** —
79	  append a `##` header with the cycle's anticipated
80	  close-out title.
81	
82	## Work-N
83	
84	The cycle's work commits (`X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, …)
85	implement the change. As many as needed:
86	
87	- Each commit runs through the
88	  **[per-commit flow](#per-commit-flow)**.
89	- **Interim pushes** are optional (backup, progress
90	  visibility).
91	- Close-out is the only mandatory push (see
92	  [Pushing](#pushing)).
93	- **Subdivide into a sub-cycle** if a Work commit's
94	  scope grows enough (see
95	  [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders)).
96	
97	## Close-out
98	
99	The cycle's last commit (bare `X.Y.Z`) does bookkeeping

35	   rules were exercised manually for the close-out;
36	   this Idea formally codifies them.
37	   - Codify the **ochid invariant** in `## ochid
38	     trailers`: every public ochid must resolve in the
39	     public graph.
40	   - Codify bot-repo rules: never squashed; descriptions
41	     editable (`jj describe` preserves chid).
42	   - Codify squash gating: until `vc-x1 push --squash`
43	     exists, manual symmetric squash (Option F: app
44	     squash + bot-side trailer rewrite + force-push) is
45	     the standard recipe; merge non-ff is the default
46	     shape for multi-commit cycles.
47	   - Sketch cross-repo migration: ochids change at
48	     every merge until the change reaches the canonical
49	     repo's `main`.
50	   - Apply Ideas-aware Preparation/Close-out updates:
51	     Preparation triages `## Ideas` (promote / fold /
52	     drop) before declaring the plan; Close-out
53	     captures unresolved follow-ups into `## Ideas`.
54	
55	## Priorities
56	
57	- P1 is highest priority, same priority are grouped equally
58	- A line with `...` is a prefix-match against a `## Todo` title
59	  - resolves locally (in this file); should be unique
60	- Entries without a prefix-match are free-form
61	  - chores tasks, ad-hoc reminders.
62	
63	### P1
64	
65	- `**vc-x1 push: support...**`
66	- `**vc-x1 push --squash...**`
67	
68	### P2
69	
70	- `**single-field...**`
71	
72	### P3
73	
74	- `**por -> dual...**`
75	
76	## Todo
77	
78	 Prioritized entries (referenced by `## Priorities`) lower priority
79	 todo's are in [todo-backlog.md](todo-backlog.md). In all cases
80	 we use the [Prose Form in CLAUDE.md](/CLAUDE.md#prose-form). When
81	 more detail is waranted those reside in `notes/chores/chores-NN.md`.
82	 Also, we use the  design subsections (link via `[N]` ref). Run
83	 `vc-x1 fix-todo --no-dry-run notes/todo.md` to renumber.
84	
85	1. **vc-x1 push: support new cycle protocol shape (N:1 code↔bot).**
86	   Today push assumes 1:1 symmetric WC commits with shared
87	   title/body. The new cycle protocol has a different shape on
88	   each side:
89	   - code side: fully committed before push (N commits via
90	     merge), nothing to commit at push time
91	   - `.claude` side: one commit with its own message (distinct
92	     from any code commit) and a multi-line `ochid:` listing
93	     all N code commits
94	
```

184	## docs: por/dual parity design (0.61.0)
185	
186	Commits: TBD (backfills at next cycle's Preparation)
187	
188	Design cycle for T5 (`por/dual parity + dual → por`).
189	Scope grew over eight commits from an audit to a full
190	CLI design — the audit doc became the canonical record
191	for both the parity divergences (where dual gets
192	privileged code paths over por) and the negotiated
193	designed surface (feature axes, decisions per axis,
194	resolution chain with baked-in default, subcommand ×
195	parameter matrix, copying mechanism for file seeding).
196	Output is input for 0.62.0+ equalization cycles. No
197	code changes land in 0.61.0 itself.
198	
199	The cycle started as "audit + ranked Todos for future
200	fixes" and grew when the audit's findings made clear
201	that defaulting topology (the user's original framing)
202	wouldn't be safe before equalizing the underlying paths,
203	and that `--por` itself was a tangled bundle of
204	independent feature axes (topology, `.vc-config.toml`
205	write, remote, privacy, copying, scaffolding) rather
206	than a single toggle. The In Progress block (moved
207	below) records the problem statement and the as-built
208	ladder.
209	
210	### Problem statement (preserved from In Progress)
211	
212	Audit where `dual` (code + `.claude/` companion, cross-
213	linked by `ochid:`) gets privileged code paths vs `por`
214	(plain single repo). Goal: produce a parity-gap document
215	that drives future parity-equalization cycles. T5 in
216	`## Todo` is the parent design item; this cycle is the
217	audit step that precedes any code-level equalization.
218	
219	The user proposed starting from `~/.config/vc-x1/config.
220	toml` to make the topology choice user-configurable; the
221	bot counter-proposed that a defaults knob shouldn't ship
222	before the underlying paths are equalized, and the user
223	agreed — audit first, equalize next, then expose
```

Open question for tracking: do we want a separate live status anywhere? Options:

1. **Leave the captured `max-review-1.md` pristine indefinitely** — rely on commits + the `todo.md > ## In Progress` ladder for "what's done." The capture stays a historical artifact.
2. **Mark items applied in `max-review-1.md`** going forward — e.g., add a `**Applied in 0.62.0-1.**` line under each finished item's Status. The captured-as-written body stays; we only append disposition.

> AGENT

Sound idea — each Concern as a -N change gives clean per-item granularity. Two clarifications before we shape Concern #1's bookkeeping:

**1. Does "move #1 to chores-12" mean delete from `max-review-1.md`, or just add a narrative bullet to chores-12?**

Earlier we restored `max-review-1.md` to pristine (after you proposed "revert change, commit so we can see changes over time"). Deleting Concern #1's section from it now would contradict that — `max-review-1.md` would shrink as items are applied, with git holding the original capture.

The alternative reading: keep `max-review-1.md` pristine, just **add** a narrative bullet to `chores-12.md > 0.62.0` recording what 0.62.0-1 accomplished.

**2. Done entry per Concern, or per cycle?**

The project convention so far is one Done entry per cycle, added at close-out (with a `[N]` ref to the chores section). The 0.61.0 close-out body even calls it out: "new ## Done entry **for the cycle**". A Done entry per Concern would diverge — possible (Done is just a rolling list), but it'd be unusual.

**3. Retroactive for Concern #1 specifically:**

0.62.0-1 is committed (unpushed) but it only did the audit-doc work, not the bookkeeping you're describing. Three ways to add bookkeeping:

- (a) **Amend 0.62.0-1** — it's still unpushed and mutable via `jj edit dbae713e`. Single coherent "do + record" commit.
- (b) **Add 0.62.0-2 as a bookkeeping commit** — separates work from record.
- (c) **Fold into the next Concern's commit** — bundle Concern #1's bookkeeping with the next item's work.

Which combination do you want?

> DEVELOPER

1. My feeling max-review is a Todo list and they should be moved (aka deleted from max-review, like we do with todo).

> AGENT

Got it — `max-review-1.md` is the cycle's todo list; applied items get **deleted** from it, and git history is the "over time" record (each deletion is a visible commit). That reconciles with your earlier "commit so we can see changes over time" — the commits *are* the record.

This means the clean shape is: **`-1` seeds the full list; each later `-N` applies one item AND deletes it from the list.** But `0.62.0-1` currently bundles *seed full list* + *apply #1/#2T audit edits* — without the deletion. Two ways to fix:

- **A. Re-split `0.62.0-1`** (it's unpushed, cheap to redo via `jj edit dbae713e`):
  - `0.62.0-1` → seed the full `max-review-1.md` only (revert the audit edits).
  - `0.62.0-2` → apply Concern #1 (3 audit spots) + delete #1 from the list + chores bullet.
  - `0.62.0-3` → apply Concern #2 Topology + trim #2 to Privacy-only + chores bullet.
  - Matches your model exactly; git shows full list → minus #1 → #2 trimmed.
- **B. Catch-up** — keep `0.62.0-1` as-is, add `0.62.0-2` that retires #1 + #2T from the list + chores bullet. Less surgery, but `-1`'s audit edits and their list-retirement live in different commits.

**Two wrinkles either way:**

1. **Concern #2 is half-done** — Topology applied, Privacy still open. It can't be fully deleted; it gets *trimmed* to a Privacy-only entry.
2. **Done entry** — I'd keep the convention of one Done entry at cycle close-out (with a `[N]` ref to the chores-12 section), and let chores-12 carry the per-item narrative. Per-Concern Done entries are possible but diverge.

I lean **A** — it's only cheap right now because `0.62.0-1` is unpushed, and it makes every later commit follow one clean rule. Which do you want?

**One thing I deferred**: no Done entry yet — I'm holding to one Done entry at cycle close-out (with a `[N]` ref to the chores section), per convention. If you'd rather have a per-Concern Done entry, say so and I'll add one now.

Next is `0.62.0-3` — Concern #2 Topology (same pattern, except #2 gets **trimmed to Privacy-only** in the review list rather than deleted, since its Privacy half is still open). Proceed?

> DEVELOPER

I suggest instead of "Exactly-one-of" either "Options --por & --dual are mutually exclusive" but actually we should probably have one option with a value --mode=por|dual

> AGENT

`--mode=por|dual` (one value-bearing flag) is the cleaner design — it dissolves the contradiction entirely (a single flag can't conflict with itself; absence → the default value), it's extensible if a third topology ever appears, and it matches the audit's already-decided "internal value is an enum, not a boolean." I'd favor it over rewording two flags.

Two things to settle before I touch the doc:

1. **Value names.** The doc's Topology *States* are `single | dual` (audit.md:508), but you wrote `por | dual`. Aligning the flag values to the state names (`--mode=single|dual`) avoids a por/single split in the vocabulary. Counter: "por" is the established term everywhere else in the doc. Also — `--mode` is generic; `--topology=` is more self-describing. So really three sub-choices: flag name (`--mode` vs `--topology`) and value names (`por|dual` vs `single|dual`).

2. **Scope / ripple.** This is bigger than Concern #2's phrasing fix. `--por`/`--dual` runs through the whole audit doc, and **Concern #1 (just applied in 0.62.0-2) uses runtime `--por`** — adopting `--mode` means the runtime override becomes `--mode=por`, which would revise text we just landed. So it's a topology-surface redesign, not a one-spot edit.

How do you want to scope `0.62.0-3`?

- **(a) Minimal** — keep two flags, reword "exactly-one-of" → "mutually exclusive (at most one; absence → `dual`)." Closes Concern #2 as the review framed it; defer `--mode` to its own item.
- **(b) Adopt `--mode=<value>`** — redesign the topology surface doc-wide, revisit Concern #1's runtime text for consistency. Lands the design you want, larger change.

I lean **(b)** since it's the design you actually want and the doc is still a pre-implementation sketch (cheap to change now) — but it's worth doing as its own tracked decision rather than smuggling it into "Concern #2 phrasing." Which way, and what flag/value names?

> DEVELOPER

(b) --mode=<value> but not sure about single vs por, I guess I lean toward single, although a short value of 1 and 2 for single dual?

> DEVELOPER

(b) --mode=<value> but not sure about single vs por, I guess I lean toward single, and then short value single=1, dual=2?

> AGENT

`single` over `por` — agreed. It matches the Topology *State* names (audit.md:508 is already `single | dual`), and it's self-documenting where `por` needs you to know the acronym. "por"/Plain Old Repo can stay as the internal/conceptual term (and the existing `PorFlag`/`FixturePor` code names); the doc should just note `--mode=single` ≡ por so the mapping is explicit.

On `single=1, dual=2` — **I'd push back.** The whole reason `--mode=<value>` beats the two-flag mess is that the value is self-describing; `--mode=2` throws that away and makes the reader memorize an arbitrary map (nothing makes single "1" and dual "2" — topology isn't an ordered scale like `-O0/-O1/-O2`). It also adds parsing surface (clap derives enums from names; numbers need custom aliases). If the goal is fewer keystrokes, better options:

- **Prefix/short aliases**: `--mode=s` / `--mode=d` (clap can accept these as enum aliases) — still mnemonic, unlike digits.
- **Keep `--por` / `--dual` as boolean shortcuts** that desugar to `--mode=single|dual` — terse *and* back-compatible with today's `--por`.

So my recommendation for the Topology surface:

- `--mode=<single|dual>`, default `dual` (absence → dual via the chain).
- Runtime `--mode=single` overrides detected topology for one invocation (restates Concern #1's target-relative rule).
- `single` ≡ por (internal term); doc notes the mapping.
- Optionally `s`/`d` aliases for brevity — *not* numbers.

One last call: flag name `--mode` vs `--topology`? `--mode` is shorter; `--topology` says what it controls. You've said `--mode` consistently, so I'll go with that unless you want the more explicit name.

Want me to write `0.62.0-3` with that shape (and `s`/`d` aliases, yes/no)?

> DEVELOPER

go

520	
521	- **Add `--dual` peer flag**. `--por` / `--dual`
522	  exactly-one-of (clap-enforced). Internal value is an
523	  enum, not a boolean — eliminates the `--por`/`!--por`
524	  framing that the audit found everywhere.
525	- **Default: `dual`** (user reversed from an earlier
526	  strict-explicit-required position once the broader
527	  "defaults where natural" principle landed). Dual is
528	  the primary use case; defaulting to it matches
529	  reality and minimizes new-user friction.
530	- **Runtime override on every subcommand** (not just
531	  `init` / `clone`). `--por` declares the target repo
532	  (`.` by default, or `-R/--repo <path>`) as single —
533	  no sibling traversal, no `[workspace] other-repo`
534	  lookup, no cross-repo ochid validation. Symmetric
535	  across code and bot — `--por` suppresses sibling
536	  discovery for whichever repo is the target, it
537	  doesn't privilege a side.
538	- **Workspace `.vc-config.toml` is dual-only**. Por
539	  never reads, never creates. Today's degenerate
540	  `[workspace] path = "/"` write under `--por` gets
541	  dropped.
542	- **Resolution chain**: CLI > ENV > Local > Global >
543	  baked-in default (`dual`). No "Error" floor for
544	  Topology — baked-in always supplies.
```

> AGENT

Core Topology Decisions block rewritten. Here's the new text for your review before I propagate:

```
- **Topology selected by `--mode=<single|dual>`** — one
  value-bearing flag, not two booleans. Replaces the
  `--por` / `--dual` peer-flag pair; a single flag can't
  conflict with itself, so the "exactly-one-of"
  enforcement disappears. Optional `s` / `d` aliases for
  brevity. Internal value is an enum — eliminates the
  `--por`/`!--por` boolean framing the audit found
  everywhere.
  - `single` (≡ por, "Plain Old Repo"): one code repo,
    no `.claude/` companion.
  - `dual`: code repo + `.claude/` companion, cross-
    linked by `ochid:`.
  - "por" stays the internal / conceptual term (and
    today's `PorFlag` / `FixturePor` code names);
    `single` is the user-facing value.
- **Default: `dual`** — absence of `--mode` → `dual` via
  the resolution chain below. ...
- **Runtime override on every subcommand** — `--mode=single`
  declares the target repo (`.` or `-R/--repo <path>`)
  as single ... At creation `--mode` sets the persistent
  topology; at runtime it overrides the detected topology
  for that invocation.
```

```
753	### Mapping `--por` / `--dual` onto axis combinations
754	
755	Today `--por` and (implicit) `--dual` are *bundle
756	shorthands*. After axes are independent, they remain as
757	shorthands for the common combinations:
758	
759	- `--dual` (or no flag — back-compat) →
760	  `(Topology=dual, Vc-config=written, Remote=github-
761	  create, Privacy=public, Copying=none)`.
762	- `--por` → `(Topology=single, Vc-config=written,
763	  Remote=github-create, Privacy=public, Copying=none)`.
764	  Note `Vc-config=written` is what today's `--por`
765	  actually does, not `not-written` (the `.vc-config.toml`
766	  is still written, just degenerate).
767	
768	A user wanting a fully-plain single repo today can't
769	spell it; tomorrow it would be `--por --config none --no-remote` (or
770	shorthand `--bare` if that combination is common enough to
771	deserve one).
772	
773	### Connections to user-config
774	
775	Once axes are independent, each maps to a `[default].*`
776	key in `~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml`, so the user-config
777	proposal that opened this cycle lands cleanly:
778	
779	- `[default].topology = "single" | "dual"`
780	- `[default].write-vc-config = true | false | "<path>"`
781	- `[default].remote = "github" | "local" | "none"`
782	- `[default].private = true | false`
```

  "new_string": "- *Creation* (init/clone): `--mode=<single|dual>` chooses\n  the workspace shape that gets written into\n  `.vc-config.toml` (dual only — single writes nothing).\n- *Runtime override* (other subcommands): `--mode=single`\n  treats the target repo (`.` by default, or\n  `-R/--repo <path>`) as single — no sibling\n  traversal, no `[workspace] other-repo` lookup.\n  Symmetric across code and bot. Default-dual at the\n  workspace level still applies (`.vc-config.toml`\n  still says dual); `--mode=single` is a per-invocation\n  override asking \"treat the target as single for\n  this command.\""
}

> AGENT

GitHub **repository visibility** at creation time — whether the repo `init`/`clone` provisions on GitHub is public or private.

From the Privacy axis (audit.md:645-668):
- **States**: `public` (default) | `private`.
- **Today's flag**: `--private`.
- **Scope**: only meaningful when Remote = `github-create` (if you're not creating a GitHub remote, it's moot).
- **Default `public`**: your explicit choice — no paid private plan + back-compat.

The Concern #2 Privacy half is that `--private` / `--public` has the same "exactly-one-of with a `[default].private` default" contradiction Topology had. The parallel fix would be one value flag.

Naming note: since this is GitHub visibility, `--visibility=<private|public>` is reasonable, but if we want to mirror `--mode`'s short style you might prefer `--vis=` or even fold it into a broader flag. Worth deciding when we actually take the Privacy half — it's still open, not something we have to settle now.

> AGENT

Not quite — per the matrix (audit.md:982-985), Privacy (`Priv`) is ✓ on **both `init` and `clone`**, and `—` (N/A) on every runtime subcommand (push, sync, finalize, chid, desc, show, list, etc.).

```
| Subcommand   | ... | Priv | ... |
| init <TARGET>| ... |  ✓   | ... |
| clone <URL>  | ... |  ✓   | ... |
| push <BM>    | ... |  —   | ... |
  ...(all runtime: —)
```

It's scoped to when Remote = `github-create`.

But your init-only instinct is worth pinning down — it's a real design question. Visibility sets a *new* GitHub repo's public/private. `init` clearly creates a new repo. `clone` clones an *existing* one (which already has its visibility). So the only way `--visibility` earns its place on `clone` is if clone ever **creates** a remote — e.g., provisioning a brand-new `.claude/` companion repo for a workspace that didn't have one. If clone only ever clones pre-existing repos, then `--visibility` on clone is dead surface and Privacy should be **init-only**.

So the question back to you: does `clone` create any remote, or only clone existing ones? If only-clone, I'd drop Privacy (and arguably the whole Remote-create axis) from the clone row. Want me to dig into `clone`'s actual behavior in the code to settle it?

> AGENT

Confirmed — you're right, **Privacy is init-only today**:

- `init -h`: `--private` ("Create private GitHub repos (default: public)").
- `clone -h`: only `--por` + utility flags — **no `--private`, no `--account`, no `--repo`**.
- `push -h`: none (expected).

And `clone` takes a `<TARGET>` to clone *from* — it clones existing repos (deriving the bot URL for dual), it doesn't **provision** new remotes. So it has no reason to set visibility.

**This means the matrix is wrong about `clone`.** audit.md:985 marks clone `✓` for both `Priv` and `A/R`:

```
| clone <URL> | ✓ creation | ✓(A/R) | ✓(Priv) | ✓* | ✓ | ✓ | — |
```

But clone today has neither, and by its nature (clone existing, don't create) it shouldn't gain them. The matrix is the "post-equalization target," so this is the target being overstated — clone's row should read `A/R —` and `Priv —` (only `T`, `CP*`, `CFG`, `NO-CFG` apply).

So two corrections fall out:

1. **Privacy half (Concern #2)** lands as `--visibility=public|private`, default `public`, **`init`-only**.

> AGENT

Clone verified — `clone.rs` has **no provisioning** (no `gh repo create`, no `--private`); it only does `jj git clone --colocate` for code and `.claude`. So it clones existing remotes, never creates them. That nails it: Privacy and Remote-create are genuinely `init`-only, and the matrix's clone row is overstated. `0.62.0-4` will fix it.

**On Cargo.toml** — agreed to bump per-commit going forward. Why it lagged: `-1/-2/-3` are purely-docs, and the protocol lets docs commits skip the cargo cycle (no install → no bump), so Cargo.toml sat at `0.62.0-0`. Bumping per-commit keeps `vc-x1 --version` honest about which `-N` you're on — the cost is running `cargo install` on each (otherwise lock/binary drift). I'll bump `-4` to `0.62.0-4`. The `-1/-2/-3` gap I'd leave (or it's moot if we squash — see below).

**On squashing — one thing to weigh before you decide.** The seed-then-delete-per-item structure we built (`-1` seeds `max-review-1.md`, `-2` deletes #1, `-3` trims #2, `-4` next…) exists *specifically* to show the review list shrinking over time in git — your original "see changes over time" goal.

- **Squash collapses that.** The single `0.62.0` commit would show `max-review-1.md` only in its final trimmed state and `audit.md` only in its final state. The per-item evolution — the whole reason for the structure — vanishes from `main`.
- **Merge non-ff** (what we did for `0.61.0`) *preserves* every `-N` commit (reachable via the merge's second parent), so `git log max-review-1.md` shows seed → −#1 → trim-#2 → … over time.

So squashing would undercut the structure we deliberately built. If the over-time record matters, merge non-ff is the consistent close-out; if you only care about a clean single entry on `main`, squash is fine but the per-item decomposition was wasted. No need to decide now — it's a close-out call — but worth knowing the per-item commits and the squash are at cross-purposes.

Proceed with `0.62.0-4` (Privacy half `--visibility=public|private` init-only + matrix clone-row fix + Cargo bump to `0.62.0-4`)?

> AGENT

Two confirmations + a convention check:

**No squash / per-item / end empty** — got it, that's consistent: merge non-ff at close-out preserves every `-N`, `max-review-1.md` shrinks to empty and gets deleted in the final step, and git (via the merge) holds the whole evolution. Good plan.

**jj auto-rebase** — yes, exactly right. `jj edit <rev>` then modifying the files rewrites that commit *and automatically rebases all descendants* onto the new version. No need to make an empty `@` first. (Afterward `@` sits *on* the edited commit, per the CLAUDE.md "post-amend" note; I'd `jj new` at the tip when done to get a clean working copy.)

> AGENT

Checked — here's the honest picture. **No hard blocker, but three things to know before I push:**

1. **Shape lock-in.** A mid-cycle push is necessarily **keep-separate** (linear) — there's no bare `0.62.0` close-out commit yet to hang a merge off. That advances `main` through `-0…-4` as individual commits. It *does* preserve every per-item commit (your no-squash goal ✓), but it **locks the shape**: doing merge-non-ff at close-out (like `0.61.0`) would then require a **force-push** to restructure already-published commits. If a keep-separate close-out is fine, no issue; if you specifically want the `0.61.0`-style trapezoid, we'd hold the push until close-out.

2. **ochid ceremony.** I committed `-0…-4` manually (`jj describe`), so **none have `ochid:` trailers**, and the `.claude` 0.62.0 session (`lontkwps`) is undescribed/unpushed. A protocol-correct push needs the manual **N:1 ceremony** (same as the `0.61.0` close-out): add `ochid: /.claude/lontkwps` to each of `-0…-4`, finalize `lontkwps` with a multi-line `ochid:` back to them, push both sides. The wrapper can't do this yet (it's Todo #1).

3. **Cargo.lock lag** at `-1/-2/-3` (toml ahead of lock) — cosmetic, matches `0.61.0` precedent, reconciles at close-out.

My recommendation: this is a backup, so **keep-separate is the right call** (merge-non-ff is a close-out nicety we don't need for an EoD safety push, and keep-separate still preserves the per-item history you wanted). I'll do the ochid + `.claude`-finalize ceremony and push both sides.

Good to proceed on that basis — or would you rather hold the push to preserve the merge-non-ff option for close-out?

> DEVELOPER

Just a linear push not a merge-no-ff and ochid should still be valid and if we squash code we'll have 2+ on the bot repo, but they can point at the same commit, agreed?

> AGENT

Agreed on all counts. The ochid invariant is just "every public ochid resolves to a public commit" — **cardinality is free in both directions**:

- **Tonight (linear push)**: `-0…-4` (5 code commits) each get `ochid: /.claude/lontkwps`, and `lontkwps` (1 bot commit) gets a multi-line `ochid:` back to all five. Many code → one bot, exactly like `0.61.0` did 9→1. ✓
- **Multiple pushes** (tonight + a close-out push) → 2+ bot commits over the cycle; each push's code commits point at that push's bot commit. All resolve. ✓
- **If we squash code later**: the squashed commit can carry a multi-line `ochid:` to whatever bot commits its work spanned; equally, 2+ bot commits can all point at the one squashed code commit. The linkage survives because resolution — not 1:1 pairing — is the only rule. ✓

So: linear/keep-separate, ochid many-to-one. Let me run the ceremony — first verify the mechanics + session chid.

What landed:
- **Code `main` → `0.62.0-4`** (`0e0da2fa`): the 5 cycle commits `-0…-4` pushed **linearly (keep-separate)**, each preserved as its own commit.
- **`.claude main` → WIP capture** (`af6c2a7f`): one bot commit with multi-line `ochid:` back to all five.
- **ochid linkage valid both ways**: `-0…-4` each → `/.claude/lontkwps` (now public); `lontkwps` → all five (now public). Invariant holds.
- Session frozen at `lontkwps`; continued session data goes to a fresh `.claude` change.

- **Shape is now locked to keep-separate.** The cycle commits are on `main`'s first-parent path, so close-out will continue linear; a `0.61.0`-style merge-non-ff would require a force-push. (Fine — keep-separate preserves the per-item history you wanted.)

> AGENT

Looked. Honest diagnosis: **nothing's broken, but you're right that I bypassed the prescribed flow.**

- **App repo**: clean — `@` is an empty change above `main` (`-4`); the "HEAD (no branch)" is just jj's normal detached-HEAD, working tree reads clean.
- **`.claude` repo**: `main → lontkwps` (pushed). But `@ = xuvstzpo` is **non-empty** — it holds the trailing session (everything since the push, including this conversation) and is **unpushed**.

The miss: I finalized `.claude` **by hand** (`jj describe` + `jj bookmark set` + `jj git push` + `jj new`) instead of using **`vc-x1 finalize`** — the tool the push wrapper's `finalize-claude` stage runs. Its whole point is the `--detach --delay`: it captures the session *including the final turn* (the chicken-and-egg you can't solve with a synchronous push, because each tool call writes more session data). By pushing synchronously, I froze `lontkwps` at the push moment and left the trailing turn in `xuvstzpo`, local-only. (For the record, the `0.61.0` push had the same hand-rolled shape — so this is a pattern worth correcting, not a one-off.)

**Proper finalize** (matches the cycle-protocol Recovery recipe):

```
vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push main --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
```

This waits, squashes the trailing `xuvstzpo` into `lontkwps` (so one bot commit holds the complete session), and pushes `main`.

Two things to flag before I run it:

1. **It force-pushes `lontkwps`** (rewrites the commit I just pushed). That's safe here: the code commits' `ochid: /.claude/lontkwps` reference the **change ID**, which is stable across the squash — so they still resolve. `.claude` is a linear journal and this is finalize's normal behavior (0.59.0 did similar).
2. **"After finalize: stop and wait"** — per the protocol, once I launch finalize I must stop and not act again until you tell me to. So I'll say goodnight in the same breath and then go quiet.

Want me to launch that exact command? (Or, if you'd rather not force-push tonight, we can leave `xuvstzpo` local — it'd fold into the next push by the journal model — but then this turn isn't backed up.)
